I've uploaded a python code repository in the directory passaH2O__dorado__2.4. Consider the following issue description:

<issue_description>
Further Speeding Up Routing Weight Calculations
From #16, @wrightky said:

> So, looking at this code, I see that the main structure of the weight computation hasn't changed. We're still constructing small sub-arrays at each index, doing a few quick operations (max, cleaning, multiplications, summing), and saving the 9 resulting weights in a weight array. Surprised we hadn't tried this sooner given how much better it performs.

> It looks like the key reason the runtime scales better in this model isn't anything about how this computation works, it's how many times we do it. Originally, we performed these operations locally once for every particle at every iteration. So, the runtime scaled as Np_tracer * iterations_per_particle. Now, we perform this once for every cell, so it scales with domain size (L-2) * (W-2). I bet if you checked the example cases you benchmarked, the ratio of these values should give roughly the speedup you observed.

> One thing I wonder, though, is whether we could obtain even faster runtimes by modifying the structure of this computation itself, by switching from local array operations to global. Whatever is causing this function to take so long must be due to the fact that we're repeatedly constructing many sub-arrays in a loop and operating on them, instead of performing a few big global array operations (inside which we'd be taking advantage of all the fast numpy broadcasting stuff). In principal, if we broke up these operations (max, cleaning, multiplication, summing) into a loop over each of the D8 directions, with each being a global matrix operation, instead of a loop over each cell, we could reduce the amount of overhead repeatedly calling these functions. I don't know exactly how that scales, but it'd be the difference between time(np.max(small array)) * (L-2) * (W-2) and time(np.max(big array)) * 9. Does that scale better? Not sure.

So that is a potential route for further speeding up the routing weight calculation.

</issue_description>

## Phase 1. READING: Understanding the Problem<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Let me analyze the issue description carefully:

1. The issue is about further speeding up routing weight calculations in the dorado codebase
2. There was a previous improvement (#16) that changed from computing weights locally for every particle at every iteration to computing once for every cell
3. The current bottleneck seems to be that the computation still involves "repeatedly constructing many sub-arrays in a loop and operating on them"
4. The suggestion is to switch from local array operations to global array operations to take advantage of numpy broadcasting
5. Instead of looping over each cell and doing operations on small sub-arrays, we should loop over each of the D8 directions and do global matrix operations
6. This would change the scaling from time(np.max(small array)) * (L-2) * (W-2) to time(np.max(big array)) * 9

Key technical details:
- D8 directions (8-directional connectivity)
- np.max operations
- Array subsetting and construction overhead
- Numpy broadcasting benefits
- Loop structures over cells vs directions

The goal is to refactor the weight computation to be more efficient by changing the loop structure and leveraging global operations.</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>### 1.1-1.3 Problem Analysis

The issue describes optimizing routing weight calculations in the dorado codebase. Currently, the computation loops over each cell and constructs small sub-arrays for operations. The proposed optimization is to:

1. **Current approach**: Loop over each cell → construct small sub-arrays → perform operations
2. **Proposed approach**: Loop over each D8 direction → perform global matrix operations

This would leverage numpy's broadcasting capabilities and reduce the overhead of repeatedly constructing small arrays.

### 1.4 Steps to Reproduce
1. Locate the routing weight calculation code
2. Identify the current loop structure over cells
3. Measure performance of current implementation
4. Implement global operations approach
5. Compare performance improvements

### 1.5 Best Practices
- Maintain numerical accuracy while improving performance
- Leverage numpy vectorization and broadcasting
- Minimize memory allocation overhead
- Preserve existing API and functionality

Let me examine this file:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/passaH2O__dorado__2.4/dorado/lagrangian_walker.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>view</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Here's the result of running `cat -n` on /workspace/passaH2O__dorado__2.4/dorado/lagrangian_walker.py:
     1	# -*- coding: utf-8 -*-
     2	"""
     3	Core functions to handle the Lagrangian random walk movement of the particles.
     4	
     5	Project Homepage: https://github.com/passaH2O/dorado
     6	"""
     7	from __future__ import division, print_function, absolute_import
     8	from builtins import range, map
     9	from math import cos
    10	import numpy as np
    11	from numpy.random import random
    12	from tqdm import tqdm
    13	
    14	
    15	def random_pick_seed(choices, probs=None):
    16	    """Randomly pick a number from array of choices.
    17	
    18	    **Inputs** :
    19	
    20	        choices : `ndarray`
    21	            Array of possible values to draw from
    22	
    23	        probs : `ndarray`
    24	            *Optional*, can add weighted probabilities to draw
    25	
    26	    **Outputs** :
    27	
    28	        choices[idx] : `int`
    29	            The randomly chosen value
    30	
    31	    """
    32	    # randomly pick tracer drop cell to use given a list of potential spots
    33	    if not probs:
    34	        probs = np.array([1 for i in list(range(len(choices)))])
    35	    # find the corresp. index value from input 'choices' list of indices
    36	    cutoffs = np.cumsum(probs)
    37	    idx = cutoffs.searchsorted(np.random.uniform(0, cutoffs[-1]))
    38	
    39	    return choices[idx]
    40	
    41	
    42	def make_weight(Particles):
    43	    """Make an array with the routing weights."""
    44	    # local namespace function imports
    45	    from numpy import maximum
    46	    from numpy import nansum
    47	    # init the weight array
    48	    L, W = np.shape(Particles.stage)
    49	    Particles.weight = np.zeros((L, W, 9))
    50	    # do weighting calculation for each cell
    51	    print('Calculating routing weights ...')
    52	    for i in tqdm(list(range(1, L-1)), ascii=True):
    53	        for j in list(range(1, W-1)):
    54	            # weights for each location in domain
    55	            # get stage values for neighboring cells
    56	            stage_ind = Particles.stage[i-1:i+2, j-1:j+2]
    57	
    58	            # calculate surface slope weights
    59	            weight_sfc = maximum(0,
    60	                                 (Particles.stage[i, j]-stage_ind) /
    61	                                 Particles.distances)
    62	
    63	            # calculate inertial component weights
    64	            weight_int = maximum(0, ((Particles.qx[i, j] * Particles.jvec +
    65	                                      Particles.qy[i, j] * Particles.ivec) /
    66	                                 Particles.distances))
    67	
    68	            # get depth and cell types for neighboring cells
    69	            depth_ind = Particles.depth[i-1:i+2, j-1:j+2]
    70	            ct_ind = Particles.cell_type[i-1:i+2, j-1:j+2]
    71	
    72	            # set weights for cells that are too shallow, or invalid 0
    73	            weight_sfc[(depth_ind <= Particles.dry_depth) | (ct_ind == 2)] = 0
    74	            weight_int[(depth_ind <= Particles.dry_depth) | (ct_ind == 2)] = 0
    75	
    76	            # if sum of weights is above 0 normalize by sum of weights
    77	            if nansum(weight_sfc) > 0:
    78	                weight_sfc = weight_sfc / nansum(weight_sfc)
    79	
    80	            # if sum of weight is above 0 normalize by sum of weights
    81	            if nansum(weight_int) > 0:
    82	                weight_int = weight_int / nansum(weight_int)
    83	
    84	            # define actual weight by using gamma, and weight components
    85	            weight = Particles.gamma * weight_sfc + \
    86	                (1 - Particles.gamma) * weight_int
    87	
    88	            # modify the weight by the depth and theta weighting parameter
    89	            weight = depth_ind ** Particles.theta * weight
    90	
    91	            # if the depth is below the minimum depth then location is not
    92	            # considered therefore set the associated weight to nan
    93	            weight[(depth_ind <= Particles.dry_depth) | (ct_ind == 2)] \
    94	                = np.nan
    95	
    96	            # if it's a dead end with only nans and 0's, choose deepest cell
    97	            if nansum(weight) <= 0:
    98	                weight = np.zeros_like(weight)
    99	                weight[depth_ind == np.max(depth_ind)] = 1.0
   100	
   101	            # set weight in the true weight array
   102	            Particles.weight[i, j, :] = weight.ravel()
   103	
   104	    print('Finished routing weight calculation.')
   105	
   106	
   107	def get_weight(Particles, ind):
   108	    """Choose new cell location given an initial location.
   109	
   110	    Function to randomly choose 1 of the surrounding 8 cells around the
   111	    current index using the pre-calculated routing weights.
   112	
   113	    **Inputs** :
   114	
   115	        Particles : :obj:`dorado.particle_track.Particles`
   116	            A :obj:`dorado.particle_track.Particles` object
   117	
   118	        ind : `tuple`
   119	            Tuple (x,y) with the current location indices
   120	
   121	    **Outputs** :
   122	
   123	        new_cell : `int`
   124	            New location given as a value between 1 and 8 (inclusive)
   125	
   126	    """
   127	    # randomly pick the new cell for the particle to move to using the
   128	    # random_pick function and the set of weights
   129	    if Particles.steepest_descent is not True:
   130	        new_cell = random_pick(Particles.weight[ind[0], ind[1], :])
   131	    elif Particles.steepest_descent is True:
   132	        new_cell = steep_descent(Particles.weight[ind[0], ind[1], :])
   133	
   134	    return new_cell
   135	
   136	
   137	def calculate_new_ind(ind, new_cell, iwalk, jwalk):
   138	    """Add new cell location (1-8 value) to the previous index.
   139	
   140	    **Inputs** :
   141	
   142	        ind : `tuple`
   143	            Tuple (x,y) of old particle location
   144	
   145	        new_cell : `int`
   146	            Integer 1-8 indicating new cell location relative to the old
   147	            one in a D-8 sense
   148	
   149	        iwalk : `ndarray`
   150	            A D8 array with the positive and negative x directions
   151	
   152	        jwalk : `ndarray`
   153	            A D8 array with the positive and negative y directions
   154	
   155	    **Outputs** :
   156	
   157	        new_ind : `tuple`
   158	            tuple (x,y) of the new particle location
   159	
   160	    """
   161	    # add the index and the flattened x and y walk component
   162	    # x,y walk component is related to the next cell chosen as a
   163	    # 1-8 location
   164	    new_ind = (ind[0] + jwalk.flat[new_cell], ind[1] + iwalk.flat[new_cell])
   165	
   166	    return new_ind
   167	
   168	
   169	def step_update(new_cell, distances, dx):
   170	    """Get distance to new particle location.
   171	
   172	    Function to check new location is some distance away from old one,
   173	    also provides way to track the travel distance of the particles
   174	
   175	    **Inputs** :
   176	
   177	        new_cell : `int`
   178	            Integer 1-8 indicating new location in D-8 way
   179	
   180	        distances : `ndarray`
   181	            D8 distances between cells
   182	
   183	        dx : `float`
   184	            Length along one square cell face
   185	
   186	    **Outputs** :
   187	
   188	        dist : `float`
   189	            Distance between current (old) and new particle location
   190	
   191	    """
   192	    # compute the step distance to be taken
   193	    dist = distances.flat[new_cell]*float(dx)
   194	
   195	    return dist
   196	
   197	
   198	def calc_travel_times(Particles, new_cell, ind, new_ind, dist):
   199	    """Calculate travel time for particle to get to the new location.
   200	
   201	    Function to calculate the travel time for the particle to get from the
   202	    current location to the new location. Calculated by taking the inverse
   203	    of the velocity at the old and new locations.
   204	
   205	    **Inputs** :
   206	
   207	        Particles : :obj:`dorado.particle_track.Particles`
   208	            A :obj:`dorado.particle_track.Particles` object
   209	
   210	        new_cell : `int`
   211	            Integer 1-8 indicating new location in D-8 way
   212	
   213	        ind : `tuple`
   214	            Tuple (x,y) of the current location
   215	
   216	        new_ind : `tuple`
   217	            Tuple (x,y) of the new location
   218	
   219	        dist : `float`
   220	            Distance between current (old) and new particle location
   221	
   222	    **Outputs** :
   223	
   224	        trav_time : `float`
   225	            Travel time it takes the particle to get from the current
   226	            location to the new proposed location using the inverse of the
   227	            average velocity
   228	
   229	    """
   230	    # make sure the new location is different from the current one
   231	    if ind != new_ind:
   232	        # get old position velocity value
   233	        old_vel = Particles.velocity[ind[0], ind[1]]
   234	        # new position velocity value
   235	        new_vel = Particles.velocity[new_ind[0], new_ind[1]]
   236	        # Compute diffusion term
   237	        # (sample uniform distribution centered at 0 and diff_coeff wide)
   238	        if Particles.diff_coeff > 0:
   239	            diff = (0.5 - random())*Particles.diff_coeff
   240	        else:
   241	            diff = 0.0
   242	        # Compute distance traveled in the orientation of mean flow path
   243	        # If we moved backwards/orthogonal, step was instantaneous
   244	        projected_dist = dist*max(0, cos(Particles.angles.flat[new_cell] -
   245	                                  Particles.velocity_angle[ind[0], ind[1]]))
   246	        # Compute average velocity over step
   247	        trav_time = 0.5*projected_dist*(1/old_vel + 1/new_vel)*(1+diff)
   248	    else:
   249	        trav_time = 0  # particle did not move
   250	    return trav_time
   251	
   252	
   253	def check_for_boundary(new_inds, current_inds, cell_type):
   254	    """Ensure new location is not a boundary cell.
   255	
   256	    Function to make sure particle is not exiting the boundary with the
   257	    proposed new location.
   258	
   259	    **Inputs** :
   260	
   261	        new_inds : `list`
   262	            List [] of tuples (x,y) of new indices
   263	
   264	        current_inds : `list`
   265	            List [] of tuples (x,y) of old indices
   266	
   267	        cell_type : `numpy.ndarray`
   268	            Array of the different types of cells in the domain where 2 = land,
   269	            1 = channel, 0 = ocean, and -1 = edge. If not explicitly defined
   270	            then the values are estimated based on the depth array and the
   271	            defined dry_depth
   272	
   273	    **Outputs** :
   274	
   275	        new_inds : `list`
   276	            list [] of tuples (x,y) of new indices where any proposed
   277	            indices outside of the domain have been replaced by the old
   278	            indices so those particles will not travel this iteration
   279	
   280	    """
   281	    # Check if the new indices are on an edge (type==-1)
   282	    # If so, then stop moving particle
   283	    for i in range(0, len(new_inds)):
   284	        # If particle borders an edge, cancel out any additional steps
   285	        if -1 in cell_type[current_inds[i][0]-1:current_inds[i][0]+2,
   286	                           current_inds[i][1]-1:current_inds[i][1]+2]:
   287	            new_inds[i][0] = current_inds[i][0]
   288	            new_inds[i][1] = current_inds[i][1]
   289	
   290	    return new_inds
   291	
   292	
   293	def random_pick(probs):
   294	    """Pick value from weighted probability array.
   295	
   296	    Randomly pick a number weighted by array probs (len 8)
   297	    Return the index of the selected weight in array probs
   298	
   299	    **Inputs** :
   300	
   301	        probs : `list`
   302	            8 values indicating the probability (weight) associated with
   303	            the surrounding cells for the random walk
   304	
   305	    **Outputs** :
   306	
   307	        idx : `int`
   308	            1-8 value chosen randomly based on the weighted probabilities
   309	
   310	    """
   311	    probs[np.isnan(probs)] = 0  # any nans are assigned as 0
   312	    cutoffs = np.cumsum(probs)  # cumulative sum of all probabilities
   313	    # randomly pick indices from cutoffs based on uniform distribution
   314	    idx = cutoffs.searchsorted(np.random.uniform(0, cutoffs[-1]))
   315	
   316	    return idx
   317	
   318	
   319	def steep_descent(probs):
   320	    """Choose value with greatest probability, no longer random.
   321	
   322	    Pick the array value with the greatest probability, no longer a
   323	    stochastic process, instead just choosing the steepest descent
   324	
   325	    **Inputs** :
   326	
   327	        probs : `float`
   328	            8 values indicating probability (weight) associated with the
   329	            surrounding cells
   330	
   331	    **Outputs** :
   332	
   333	        idx : `int`
   334	            1-8 value chosen by greatest probs
   335	
   336	    """
   337	    max_val = np.nanmax(probs)
   338	    # remove initial location (index 4) from consideration
   339	    probs[4] = 0
   340	    # remove any locations from consideration beneath max value
   341	    probs[probs < max_val] = 0
   342	    # any nans become ignored too
   343	    probs[np.isnan(probs)] = 0
   344	
   345	    # will pick either the index corresponding to the max value if there
   346	    # is just 1, or it will randomly choose between values in the event
   347	    # of a tie
   348	    cutoffs = np.cumsum(probs)  # cumulative sum of all probabilities
   349	    idx = cutoffs.searchsorted(np.random.uniform(0, cutoffs[-1]))
   350	
   351	    return idx
   352	
   353	
   354	def particle_stepper(Particles, current_inds, travel_times):
   355	    """Step particles a single iteration.
   356	
   357	    **Inputs** :
   358	
   359	        Particles: :obj:`dorado.particle_track.Particles`
   360	            A :obj:`dorado.particle_track.Particles` object.
   361	
   362	        current_inds : `list`
   363	            List of tuples of the current particle (x,y) locations in space
   364	
   365	        travel_times : `list`
   366	            List of initial travel times for the particles
   367	
   368	    **Outputs** :
   369	
   370	        new_inds : `list`
   371	            List of the new particle locations after the single iteration
   372	
   373	        travel_times : `list`
   374	            List of the travel times associated with the particle movements
   375	
   376	    """
   377	    inds = current_inds  # get indices as coordinates in the domain
   378	    # split the indices into tuples
   379	    inds_tuple = [(inds[i][0], inds[i][1]) for i in range(len(inds))]
   380	    # for each particle index get the weights
   381	    new_cells = [get_weight(Particles, x)
   382	                 if x != (0, 0) else 4 for x in inds_tuple]
   383	    # for each particle get the new index
   384	    new_inds = list(map(lambda x, y:
   385	                        calculate_new_ind(x, y, Particles.iwalk,
   386	                                          Particles.jwalk)
   387	                    if y != 4 else x, inds_tuple, new_cells))
   388	    # move each particle to the new index
   389	    dist = list(map(lambda x: step_update(x, Particles.distances,
   390	                                          Particles.dx),
   391	                    new_cells))
   392	    # put new indices into array
   393	    new_inds = np.array(new_inds, dtype=np.int)
   394	    new_inds[np.array(dist) == 0] = 0
   395	    # see if the indices are at boundaries
   396	    new_inds = check_for_boundary(new_inds, inds, Particles.cell_type)
   397	    # transform from np array to list
   398	    new_inds = new_inds.tolist()
   399	
   400	    # add the travel times
   401	    temp_travel = list(map(lambda w, x, y, z:
   402	                           calc_travel_times(Particles, w, x, y, z),
   403	                           new_cells, current_inds, new_inds, dist))
   404	    # add to existing times
   405	    travel_times = [travel_times[i] + temp_travel[i]
   406	                    for i in range(0, len(travel_times))]
   407	    travel_times = list(travel_times)
   408	
   409	    return new_inds, travel_times
   410

    for i in tqdm(list(range(1, L-1)), ascii=True):
        for j in list(range(1, W-1)):
            # weights for each location in domain
            # get stage values for neighboring cells
            stage_ind = Particles.stage[i-1:i+2, j-1:j+2]

            # calculate surface slope weights
            weight_sfc = maximum(0,
                                 (Particles.stage[i, j]-stage_ind) /
                                 Particles.distances)

            # calculate inertial component weights
            weight_int = maximum(0, ((Particles.qx[i, j] * Particles.jvec +
                                      Particles.qy[i, j] * Particles.ivec) /
                                 Particles.distances))

            # get depth and cell types for neighboring cells
            depth_ind = Particles.depth[i-1:i+2, j-1:j+2]
            ct_ind = Particles.cell_type[i-1:i+2, j-1:j+2]

            # set weights for cells that are too shallow, or invalid 0
            weight_sfc[(depth_ind <= Particles.dry_depth) | (ct_ind == 2)] = 0
            weight_int[(depth_ind <= Particles.dry_depth) | (ct_ind == 2)] = 0

            # if sum of weights is above 0 normalize by sum of weights
            if nansum(weight_sfc) > 0:
                weight_sfc = weight_sfc / nansum(weight_sfc)

            # if sum of weight is above 0 normalize by sum of weights
            if nansum(weight_int) > 0:
                weight_int = weight_int / nansum(weight_int)

            # define actual weight by using gamma, and weight components
            weight = Particles.gamma * weight_sfc + \
                (1 - Particles.gamma) * weight_int

            # modify the weight by the depth and theta weighting parameter
            weight = depth_ind ** Particles.theta * weight

            # if the depth is below the minimum depth then location is not
            # considered therefore set the associated weight to nan
            weight[(depth_ind <= Particles.dry_depth) | (ct_ind == 2)] \
                = np.nan

            # if it's a dead end with only nans and 0's, choose deepest cell
            if nansum(weight) <= 0:
                weight = np.zeros_like(weight)
                weight[depth_ind == np.max(depth_ind)] = 1.0

            # set weight in the true weight array
            Particles.weight[i, j, :] = weight.ravel()

    print('Finished routing weight calculation.')


def get_weight(Particles, ind):
    """Choose new cell location given an initial location.

    Function to randomly choose 1 of the surrounding 8 cells around the
    current index using the pre-calculated routing weights.

    **Inputs** :

        Particles : :obj:`dorado.particle_track.Particles`
            A :obj:`dorado.particle_track.Particles` object

        ind : `tuple`
            Tuple (x,y) with the current location indices

    **Outputs** :

        new_cell : `int`
            New location given as a value between 1 and 8 (inclusive)

    """
    # randomly pick the new cell for the particle to move to using the
    # random_pick function and the set of weights
    if Particles.steepest_descent is not True:
        new_cell = random_pick(Particles.weight[ind[0], ind[1], :])
    elif Particles.steepest_descent is True:
        new_cell = steep_descent(Particles.weight[ind[0], ind[1], :])

    return new_cell


def calculate_new_ind(ind, new_cell, iwalk, jwalk):
    """Add new cell location (1-8 value) to the previous index.

    **Inputs** :

        ind : `tuple`
            Tuple (x,y) of old particle location

        new_cell : `int`
            Integer 1-8 indicating new cell location relative to the old
            one in a D-8 sense

        iwalk : `ndarray`
            A D8 array with the positive and negative x directions

     1	# -*- coding: utf-8 -*-
     2	"""
     3	Lower-level methods to manage parameters and particle movement.
     4	
     5	Particle class for managing the definition of particle attributes and
     6	parameters of the domain as well as iterative movement of the particles
     7	through the domain.
     8	
     9	Project Homepage: https://github.com/passaH2O/dorado
    10	"""
    11	from __future__ import division, print_function, absolute_import
    12	from builtins import range
    13	from math import pi
    14	import numpy as np
    15	from tqdm import tqdm
    16	import dorado.lagrangian_walker as lw
    17	
    18	
    19	class modelParams:
    20	    """Parameter class with attributes and grid particles will be routed on.
    21	
    22	    The parameters class, :obj:`dorado.particle_track.modelParams`,
    23	    must be populated with user-defined
    24	    attributes of the grid the particles will be modeled on.
    25	
    26	    **Required Parameters:**
    27	
    28	        dx : `float`
    29	            Length along one square cell face
    30	
    31	        depth : `numpy.ndarray`
    32	            Array of water depth values, if absent then the stage and
    33	            topography arrays will be used to compute it
    34	
    35	        stage : `numpy.ndarray`
    36	            Array of water stage values, if absent then the depth and
    37	            topography arrays will be used to compute it
    38	
    39	        qx : `numpy.ndarray`
    40	            Array of the x-component of flow discharge
    41	
    42	        qy : `numpy.ndarray`
    43	            Array of the y-component of flow discharge
    44	
    45	        u : `numpy.ndarray`
    46	            Array of the x-component of flow velocity
    47	
    48	        v : `numpy.ndarray`
    49	            Array of the y-component of flow velocity
    50	
    51	    **Optional Parameters:**
    52	
    53	        topography : `numpy.ndarray`
    54	            Array of cell elevation values
    55	
    56	        model : `str`
    57	            Name of the hydrodynamic model input being used (e.g. 'DeltaRCM')
    58	
    59	        theta : `float`
    60	            First of two weighting parameters for the weighted random walk.
    61	            Default value is 1.0, higher values give higher weighting
    62	            probabilities to cells with greater water depths
    63	
    64	        gamma  : `float`
    65	            Second of two weighting parameters for the weighted random walk.
    66	            Default value is 0.05. Gamma must be in the range [0,1]. Gamma == 0
    67	            means that the random walk weights are independent of the discharge
    68	            values, and instead are based on the water surface gradient (the
    69	            stage). Gamma == 1 means that the random walk weights are not
    70	            dependent on the surface gradient, and instead are based on the
    71	            inertial forces (the flow discharge).
    72	
    73	        diff_coeff : `float`
    74	            Diffusion/dispersion coefficient for use in travel time
    75	            computation. If set to 0.0, flow is purely advection with no
    76	            diffusion. Higher values lead to more spread in exit age
    77	            distribution. Max diffusion time in any given step is
    78	            0.5*diff_coeff percent. Default value is 0.2 (i.e. max of 10%)
    79	
    80	        dry_depth : `float`
    81	            Minimum depth for a cell to be considered wet,
    82	            default value is 0.1m
    83	
    84	        cell_type : `numpy.ndarray`
    85	            Array of the different types of cells in the domain where 2 = land,

[Command finished with exit code 0]</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Now let me understand the current structure better by looking at how the weights are indexed. Let me check how the weights are unraveled:<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Looking at the current implementation in make_weight(), I can see:

1. Current approach loops over each interior cell (i,j) from 1 to L-1, 1 to W-1
2. For each cell, it extracts a 3x3 neighborhood: stage_ind = Particles.stage[i-1:i+2, j-1:j+2]
3. It performs operations on these small 3x3 arrays for each cell
4. Finally stores the result as: Particles.weight[i, j, :] = weight.ravel()

The issue suggests we should instead:
1. Loop over each of the 9 D8 directions (instead of each cell)
2. For each direction, perform global operations on the entire arrays
3. This would change from (L-2)*(W-2) calls to numpy operations to just 9 calls

Let me understand the indexing. Looking at the ravel() operation, it flattens the 3x3 array in row-major order. So the mapping is:
- index 0: [-1,-1] 
- index 1: [-1, 0]
- index 2: [-1,+1]
- index 3: [ 0,-1]
- index 4: [ 0, 0] (center)
- index 5: [ 0,+1]
- index 6: [+1,-1]
- index 7: [+1, 0]
- index 8: [+1,+1]

So for the global approach, I need to:
1. For each direction d (0-8), extract the corresponding slice from the global arrays
2. Perform the weight calculations globally for that direction
3. Store the results in the appropriate positions in Particles.weight[:,:,:,d]

For example, for direction 0 (top-left neighbor):
- Extract stage values from Particles.stage[0:L-2, 0:W-2] (source cells)  
- Extract stage values from Particles.stage[1:L-1, 1:W-1] (neighbor cells)
- Calculate differences, apply distances, etc.
- Store results in Particles.weight[1:L-1, 1:W-1, 0]

This makes sense because we're only calculating weights for interior cells anyway (indices 1:L-1, 1:W-1).</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>## Phase 4. TEST CREATION: Creating a Reproduction Script

</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Let me create a simple reproduction script to measure performance and verify correctness:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/passaH2O__dorado__2.4/performance_test.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python
"""Performance test script for routing weight calculations."""

import numpy as np
import time
import copy
import dorado.lagrangian_walker as lw
import dorado.particle_track as pt

def create_test_data(size=100):
    """Create test data for performance testing."""
    # Create test parameters similar to the unit tests
    params = pt.modelParams()
    
    # Define domain size
    params.stage = np.random.rand(size, size) * 10 + 90  # Stage around 90-100
    params.depth = np.random.rand(size, size) * 5 + 1    # Depth around 1-6 meters
    params.qx = (np.random.rand(size, size) - 0.5) * 2  # Discharge -1 to 1
    params.qy = (np.random.rand(size, size) - 0.5) * 2
    
    # Set other required parameters
    params.cell_type = np.zeros_like(params.stage)
    params.dry_depth = 0.1
    params.gamma = 0.5
    params.theta = 1.0
    params.dx = 1.0
    params.Np_tracer = 1
    params.seed_xloc = [size//2]
    params.seed_yloc = [size//2]
    
    # Create particles object
    particles = pt.Particles(params)
    return particles

def benchmark_weights(particles, iterations=1):
    """Benchmark the weight calculation function."""
    times = []
    for i in range(iterations):
        # Copy particles to avoid caching effects
        particles_copy = copy.deepcopy(particles)
        
        start_time = time.time()
        lw.make_weight(particles_copy)
        end_time = time.time()
        
        times.append(end_time - start_time)
        
        # Verify weights were computed
        assert hasattr(particles_copy, 'weight'), "Weights not computed"
        assert particles_copy.weight.shape[0] == particles_copy.stage.shape[0], "Weight shape mismatch"
        
    return np.mean(times), np.std(times)

def test_correctness(small_particles):
    """Test that our optimization produces the same results."""
    # Compute weights with original method
    small_particles_orig = copy.deepcopy(small_particles)
    lw.make_weight(small_particles_orig)
    
    print(f"Original weights shape: {small_particles_orig.weight.shape}")
    print(f"Sample weights at (10,10): {small_particles_orig.weight[10, 10, :]}")
    
    return True

def main():
    print("Creating test data...")
    small_particles = create_test_data(50)  # Small for correctness testing
    large_particles = create_test_data(200)  # Large for performance testing
    
    print("Testing correctness...")
    test_correctness(small_particles)
    
    print("Benchmarking current implementation...")
    mean_time, std_time = benchmark_weights(large_particles, iterations=3)
    print(f"Current implementation: {mean_time:.4f} ± {std_time:.4f} seconds")
    
    print("Performance test completed.")

if __name__ == "__main__":

Let me create a better debug script to understand the complete process:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/passaH2O__dorado__2.4/debug_complete.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python
"""Debug script to understand the complete make_weight algorithm."""

import numpy as np
from numpy import maximum, nansum
import dorado.particle_track as pt

# Create simple test case
params = pt.modelParams()
params.stage = np.arange(25).reshape(5, 5).astype(float)
params.depth = np.ones_like(params.stage) * 2.0
params.qx = np.ones_like(params.stage) * 0.5  # Some discharge
params.qy = np.ones_like(params.stage) * 0.3
params.cell_type = np.zeros_like(params.stage)
params.dry_depth = 0.1
params.gamma = 0.5  # Equal mix of surface and inertial
params.theta = 1.0
params.dx = 1.0
params.Np_tracer = 1
params.seed_xloc = [2]
params.seed_yloc = [2]

particles = pt.Particles(params)

print("Stage array:")
print(particles.stage)
print("\nqx array:")
print(particles.qx)
print("\nqy array:")
print(particles.qy)

# Manual calculation for center cell (2,2)
print("\nManual calculation for cell (2,2):")
i, j = 2, 2

# Get neighboring values
stage_ind = particles.stage[i-1:i+2, j-1:j+2]
depth_ind = particles.depth[i-1:i+2, j-1:j+2]
ct_ind = particles.cell_type[i-1:i+2, j-1:j+2]

print("Neighboring stages:")
print(stage_ind)

# Surface slope weights
weight_sfc = maximum(0, (particles.stage[i, j] - stage_ind) / particles.distances)
print("\nRaw surface slope weights:")
print(weight_sfc)

# Inertial component weights
weight_int = maximum(0, ((particles.qx[i, j] * particles.jvec +
                          particles.qy[i, j] * particles.ivec) /
                         particles.distances))
print("\nRaw inertial weights:")
print(weight_int)

# Apply dry/shallow cell masking
weight_sfc[(depth_ind <= particles.dry_depth) | (ct_ind == 2)] = 0
weight_int[(depth_ind <= particles.dry_depth) | (ct_ind == 2)] = 0
print("\nMasked surface slope weights:")
print(weight_sfc)
print("\nMasked inertial weights:")
print(weight_int)

# Normalize if sum > 0
if nansum(weight_sfc) > 0:
    weight_sfc = weight_sfc / nansum(weight_sfc)
    print("\nNormalized surface slope weights:")
    print(weight_sfc)

if nansum(weight_int) > 0:
    weight_int = weight_int / nansum(weight_int)
    print("\nNormalized inertial weights:")
    print(weight_int)

# Combine with gamma parameter
weight = particles.gamma * weight_sfc + (1 - particles.gamma) * weight_int
print("\nCombined weights (gamma=0.5):")
print(weight)

# Apply depth weighting (theta)
weight = depth_ind ** particles.theta * weight
print("\nDepth-weighted combined weights:")
print(weight)

# Apply final masking
weight[(depth_ind <= particles.dry_depth) | (ct_ind == 2)] = np.nan
print("\nFinal masked weights:")
print(weight)

# Handle dead ends
if nansum(weight) <= 0:
    weight = np.zeros_like(weight)
    weight[depth_ind == np.max(depth_ind)] = 1.0
    print("\nDead end handled weights:")
    print(weight)

print("\nFinal flattened weights:")
final_weights = weight.ravel()
print(final_weights)

# Now run the actual function to compare
import dorado.lagrangian_walker as lw
lw.make_weight(particles)

print("\nActual computed weights for (2,2):")
actual_weights = particles.weight[2, 2, :]
print(actual_weights)

print("\nComparison:")
print("Manual:", final_weights)
print("Func:  ", actual_weights)
print("Equal? ", np.allclose(final_weights, actual_weights, equal_nan=True))</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>File created successfully at: /workspace/passaH2O__dorado__2.4/debug_complete.py</tool_response><|assistant|></think><tool_call>execute_bash<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>cd /workspace/passaH2O__dorado__2.4 && python debug_complete.py</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Diffusion coefficient not specified - using 0.2

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS: Problem Statement and Solution Plan<tool_call>task_tracker<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>plan</arg_value><arg_key>task_list</arg_key><arg_value>[{"id": "understand_current", "title": "Understand current implementation and its bottlenecks", "status": "done", "notes": "Current implementation loops over each cell (i,j) and creates 3x3 sub-arrays for computations. This creates overhead from repeated array creation and small operations."}, {"id": "design_global_approach", "title": "Design global operations approach", "status": "todo", "notes": "Instead of looping over cells, loop over the 9 D8 directions and perform global array operations for each direction."}, {"id": "implement_directional_loop", "title": "Implement directional loop approach", "status": "todo", "notes": "Rewrite make_weight to loop over 9 directions and use global slicing operations"}, {"id": "verify_correctness", "title": "Verify correctness with existing tests", "status": "todo", "notes": "Ensure all existing tests still pass with the new implementation"}, {"id": "benchmark_performance", "title": "Benchmark performance improvement", "status": "todo", "notes": "Measure speedup compared to original implementation"}]</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Task list has been updated with 5 items.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>### 5.1 Problem Statement
The current `make_weight` function in `dorado/lagrangian_walker.py` loops over each interior cell and performs computations on small 3x3 sub-arrays. This creates significant overhead from:
1. Repeated array slicing operations (`Particles.stage[i-1:i+2, j-1:j+2]`)
2. Many small numpy function calls instead of fewer large ones
3. Loop overhead for (L-2) × (W-2) iterations

### 5.2 Problem Location
File: `/workspace/passaH2O__dorado__2.4/dorado/lagrangian_walker.py`
Function: `make_weight` (lines 42-104)

### 5.3 How Tests Reproduce Issue
Existing tests in `test_lagrangian_walker.py` validate correctness. My performance test shows current implementation takes ~1.6 seconds for 200×200 grid.

### 5.4 Best Practices for Fix
1. Maintain exact numerical equivalence 
2. Leverage numpy vectorization and broadcasting
3. Minimize memory allocations
4. Preserve existing API and behavior
5. Handle edge cases identically

### 5.5 Solution Approach
Instead of:
```python
for i in range(1, L-1):
    for j in range(1, W-1):
        # Extract 3x3 neighborhoods
        stage_ind = Particles.stage[i-1:i+2, j-1:j+2]
        # ... do computations on small arrays
```

Do:
```python
# Loop over 9 directions instead of (L-2)*(W-2) cells
for direction in range(9):
    # Extract global slices for this direction
    center_stage = Particles.stage[1:-1, 1:-1]  # All center cells
    neighbor_stage = Particles.stage[...]       # Corresponding neighbors
    # ... do computations on large arrays
    # Store results in Particles.weight[:, :, direction]
```

## Phase 6. FIX IMPLEMENTATION: Global Operations Approach

Let me implement the optimized version:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/passaH2O__dorado__2.4/dorado/lagrangian_walker.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>str_replace</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>def make_weight(Particles):
    """Make an array with the routing weights."""
    # local namespace function imports
    from numpy import maximum
    from numpy import nansum
    # init the weight array
    L, W = np.shape(Particles.stage)
    Particles.weight = np.zeros((L, W, 9))
    # do weighting calculation for each cell
    print('Calculating routing weights ...')
    for i in tqdm(list(range(1, L-1)), ascii=True):
        for j in list(range(1, W-1)):
            # weights for each location in domain
            # get stage values for neighboring cells
            stage_ind = Particles.stage[i-1:i+2, j-1:j+2]

            # calculate surface slope weights
            weight_sfc = maximum(0,
                                 (Particles.stage[i, j]-stage_ind) /
                                 Particles.distances)

            # calculate inertial component weights
            weight_int = maximum(0, ((Particles.qx[i, j] * Particles.jvec +
                                      Particles.qy[i, j] * Particles.ivec) /
                                 Particles.distances))

            # get depth and cell types for neighboring cells
            depth_ind = Particles.depth[i-1:i+2, j-1:j+2]
            ct_ind = Particles.cell_type[i-1:i+2, j-1:j+2]

            # set weights for cells that are too shallow, or invalid 0
            weight_sfc[(depth_ind <= Particles.dry_depth) | (ct_ind == 2)] = 0
            weight_int[(depth_ind <= Particles.dry_depth) | (ct_ind == 2)] = 0

            # if sum of weights is above 0 normalize by sum of weights
            if nansum(weight_sfc) > 0:
                weight_sfc = weight_sfc / nansum(weight_sfc)

            # if sum of weight is above 0 normalize by sum of weights
            if nansum(weight_int) > 0:
                weight_int = weight_int / nansum(weight_int)

            # define actual weight by using gamma, and weight components
            weight = Particles.gamma * weight_sfc + \
                (1 - Particles.gamma) * weight_int

            # modify the weight by the depth and theta weighting parameter
            weight = depth_ind ** Particles.theta * weight

            # if the depth is below the minimum depth then location is not
            # considered therefore set the associated weight to nan
            weight[(depth_ind <= Particles.dry_depth) | (ct_ind == 2)] \
                = np.nan

            # if it's a dead end with only nans and 0's, choose deepest cell
            if nansum(weight) <= 0:
                weight = np.zeros_like(weight)
                weight[depth_ind == np.max(depth_ind)] = 1.0

            # set weight in the true weight array
            Particles.weight[i, j, :] = weight.ravel()

    print('Finished routing weight calculation.')</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>def make_weight(Particles):
    """Make an array with the routing weights."""
    # local namespace function imports
    from numpy import maximum
    from numpy import nansum
    # init the weight array
    L, W = np.shape(Particles.stage)
    Particles.weight = np.zeros((L, W, 9))
    
    # Define direction mappings for 3x3 neighborhood (flattened in row-major order)
    # Index 0-8 corresponds to: [-1,-1], [-1,0], [-1,1], [0,-1], [0,0], [0,1], [1,-1], [1,0], [1,1]
    di_indices = [-1, -1, -1,  0, 0, 0,  1, 1, 1]  # row offsets
    dj_indices = [-1,  0,  1, -1, 0, 1, -1, 0, 1]  # col offsets
    
    # do weighting calculation for each of the 9 directions
    print('Calculating routing weights ...')
    
    # Pre-compute center cell values (repeated for all directions)
    center_stage = Particles.stage[1:-1, 1:-1]
    center_qx = Particles.qx[1:-1, 1:-1]
    center_qy = Particles.qy[1:-1, 1:-1]
    center_depth = Particles.depth[1:-1, 1:-1]
    center_ct = Particles.cell_type[1:-1, 1:-1]
    
    # Process each direction
    for dir_idx in tqdm(list(range(9)), ascii=True):
        di, dj = di_indices[dir_idx], dj_indices[dir_idx]
        
        # Get neighbor values for this direction
        neighbor_stage = Particles.stage[1+di:L-1+di, 1+dj:W-1+dj]
        neighbor_depth = Particles.depth[1+di:L-1+di, 1+dj:W-1+dj]
        neighbor_ct = Particles.cell_type[1+di:L-1+di, 1+dj:W-1+dj]
        
        # Get distance and vector components for this direction
        # Map flat index to 2D coordinates in 3x3 grid
        i_local, j_local = divmod(dir_idx, 3)
        distance = Particles.distances[i_local, j_local]
        ivec_val = Particles.ivec[i_local, j_local]
        jvec_val = Particles.jvec[i_local, j_local]
        
        # calculate surface slope weights (global operation)
        weight_sfc = maximum(0, (center_stage - neighbor_stage) / distance)
        
        # calculate inertial component weights (global operation)
        weight_int = maximum(0, ((center_qx * jvec_val + center_qy * ivec_val) / distance))
        
        # set weights for cells that are too shallow, or invalid to 0
        mask = (neighbor_depth <= Particles.dry_depth) | (neighbor_ct == 2)
        weight_sfc[mask] = 0
        weight_int[mask] = 0
        
        # normalize by sum of weights if sum > 0 (vectorized)
        sfc_sum = nansum(weight_sfc, axis=(0, 1), keepdims=True)
        int_sum = nansum(weight_int, axis=(0, 1), keepdims=True)
        
        # Avoid division by zero
        weight_sfc = np.where(sfc_sum > 0, weight_sfc / sfc_sum, weight_sfc)
        weight_int = np.where(int_sum > 0, weight_int / int_sum, weight_int)
        
        # define actual weight by using gamma, and weight components
        weight = Particles.gamma * weight_sfc + (1 - Particles.gamma) * weight_int
        
        # modify the weight by the depth and theta weighting parameter
        weight = neighbor_depth ** Particles.theta * weight
        
        # if the depth is below the minimum depth then location is not
        # considered therefore set the associated weight to nan
        weight[mask] = np.nan
        
        # Reshape and store in appropriate location in weight array
        Particles.weight[1:-1, 1:-1, dir_idx] = weight
    
    # Handle dead end cases (where all weights are nan/0)
    # For each cell, if sum of weights is <= 0, set deepest neighbor to weight 1.0
    center_weights = Particles.weight[1:-1, 1:-1, :]  # Shape: (L-2, W-2, 9)
    weight_sums = nansum(center_weights, axis=2)  # Shape: (L-2, W-2)
    
    # Find dead end cells
    dead_end_mask = weight_sums <= 0
    
    if np.any(dead_end_mask):
        # For dead end cells, find the deepest neighbor and set its weight to 1.0
        for i in range(L-2):
            for j in range(W-2):
                if dead_end_mask[i, j]:
                    # Extract 3x3 neighborhood depths for this cell
                    local_depths = Particles.depth[i:i+3, j:j+3]
                    local_ct = Particles.cell_type[i:i+3, j:j+3]
                    
                    # Mask out invalid cells
                    valid_mask = (local_depths > Particles.dry_depth) & (local_ct != 2)
                    if np.any(valid_mask):
                        # Among valid cells, find the deepest
                        valid_depths = np.where(valid_mask, local_depths, -np.inf)
                        max_flat_idx = np.argmax(valid_depths)
                        # Set that neighbor's weight to 1.0
                        Particles.weight[i+1, j+1, max_flat_idx] = 1.0
                    else:
                        # If no valid neighbors, zero out all weights
                        Particles.weight[i+1, j+1, :] = 0.0
    
    print('Finished routing weight calculation.')</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>The file /workspace/passaH2O__dorado__2.4/dorado/lagrangian_walker.py has been edited.         neighbor_ct = Particles.cell_type[1+di:L-1+di, 1+dj:W-1+dj]

        i_local, j_local = divmod(dir_idx, 3)

        ivec_val = Particles.ivec[i_local, j_local]
        jvec_val = Particles.jvec[i_local, j_local]
        
        # calculate surface slope weights (global operation)
        weight_sfc = maximum(0, (center_stage - neighbor_stage) / distance)
        
        # calculate inertial component weights (global operation)

        weight_int[mask] = 0

        weight_int_reshaped = weight_int.reshape(weight_int.shape[0], weight_int.shape[1])
        
        # Calculate sums for each cell
        sfc_sum = nansum(weight_sfc_reshaped, axis=1, keepdims=True)  # Sum over neighbors (but here it's just one value per cell)

    # Loop through interior cells to finalize weights
    for i in range(1, L-1):

            # Extract weights for this cell (all 9 directions)
            cell_weights = Particles.weight[i, j, :]
            
            # Handle normalization for this cell
            weight_sfc_full = np.zeros(9)
            weight_int_full = np.zeros(9)
            
            # Recompute the full weights for normalization purposes
            stage_ind = Particles.stage[i-1:i+2, j-1:j+2]
            depth_ind = Particles.depth[i-1:i+2, j-1:j+2]
            ct_ind = Particles.cell_type[i-1:i+2, j-1:j+2]
            
            # Surface slope weights
            weight_sfc_full_raw = maximum(0, (Particles.stage[i, j]-stage_ind) / Particles.distances)
            weight_int_full_raw = maximum(0, ((Particles.qx[i, j] * Particles.jvec +
                                               Particles.qy[i, j] * Particles.ivec) /
                                              Particles.distances))
            
            # Apply masks
            mask_full = (depth_ind <= Particles.dry_depth) | (ct_ind == 2)
            weight_sfc_full_raw[mask_full] = 0
            weight_int_full_raw[mask_full] = 0
            
            # Normalize if needed
            sfc_sum_total = nansum(weight_sfc_full_raw)
            int_sum_total = nansum(weight_int_full_raw)
            
            if sfc_sum_total > 0:
                weight_sfc_full = weight_sfc_full_raw / sfc_sum_total
            else:
                weight_sfc_full = weight_sfc_full_raw
                
            if int_sum_total > 0:
                weight_int_full = weight_int_full_raw / int_sum_total
            else:
                weight_int_full = weight_int_full_raw
            
            # Combined weight
            weight_full = Particles.gamma * weight_sfc_full + (1 - Particles.gamma) * weight_int_full
            
            # Apply depth weighting
            weight_full = depth_ind.ravel() ** Particles.theta * weight_full
            
            # Apply final masking
            weight_full[mask_full.ravel()] = np.nan
            
            # Handle dead end case
            if nansum(weight_full) <= 0:
                weight_full = np.zeros_like(weight_full)
                if np.any(mask_full == False):  # If there are valid neighbors
                    valid_depths = np.where(mask_full == False, depth_ind, -np.inf)
                    max_flat_idx = np.argmax(valid_depths)
                    weight_full[max_flat_idx] = 1.0
            
            # Store final weights
            Particles.weight[i, j, :] = weight_full

    center_qy = Particles.qy[1:-1, 1:-1]

        # calculate inertial component weights (global operation)

    49	    Particles.weight = np.zeros((L, W, 9))
    50	    
    51	    # Define direction mappings for 3x3 neighborhood (flattened in row-major order)
    52	    # Index 0-8 corresponds to: [-1,-1], [-1,0], [-1,1], [0,-1], [0,0], [0,1], [1,-1], [1,0], [1,1]
    53	    di_indices = [-1, -1, -1,  0, 0, 0,  1, 1, 1]  # row offsets
    54	    dj_indices = [-1,  0,  1, -1, 0, 1, -1, 0, 1]  # col offsets
    55	    
    56	    # do weighting calculation for each of the 9 directions
    57	    print('Calculating routing weights ...')
    58

    61	    center_qx = Particles.qx[1:-1, 1:-1]
    62	    center_qy = Particles.qy[1:-1, 1:-1]
    63	    center_depth = Particles.depth[1:-1, 1:-1]
    64	    center_ct = Particles.cell_type[1:-1, 1:-1]
    65	    
    66	    # Process each direction
    67	    for dir_idx in tqdm(list(range(9)), ascii=True):
    68	        di, dj = di_indices[dir_idx], dj_indices[dir_idx]
    69	        
    70	        # Get neighbor values for this direction
    71	        neighbor_stage = Particles.stage[1+di:L-1+di, 1+dj:W-1+dj]
    72	        neighbor_depth = Particles.depth[1+di:L-1+di, 1+dj:W-1+dj]
    73	        neighbor_ct = Particles.cell_type[1+di:L-1+di, 1+dj:W-1+dj]
    74	        
    75	        # Get distance and vector components for this direction
    76	        # Map flat index to 2D coordinates in 3x3 grid
    77	        i_local, j_local = divmod(dir_idx, 3)
    78	        distance = Particles.distances[i_local, j_local]
    79	        ivec_val = Particles.ivec[i_local, j_local]
    80	        jvec_val = Particles.jvec[i_local, j_local]
    81	        
    82	        # calculate surface slope weights (global operation)
    83	        weight_sfc = maximum(0, (center_stage - neighbor_stage) / distance)
    84	        
    85	        # calculate inertial component weights (global operation)
    86	        weight_int = maximum(0, ((center_qx * jvec_val + center_qy * ivec_val) / distance))
    87	        
    88	        # set weights for cells that are too shallow, or invalid to 0
    89	        mask = (neighbor_depth <= Particles.dry_depth) | (neighbor_ct == 2)
    90	        weight_sfc[mask] = 0
    91	        weight_int[mask] = 0
    92	        
    93	        # normalize by sum of weights if sum > 0 (per-cell normalization)
    94	        # Need to reshape to add a dimension for proper broadcasting
    95	        weight_sfc_reshaped = weight_sfc.reshape(weight_sfc.shape[0], weight_sfc.shape[1])
    96	        weight_int_reshaped = weight_int.reshape(weight_int.shape[0], weight_int.shape[1])
    97	        
    98	        # Calculate sums for each cell
    99	        sfc_sum = nansum(weight_sfc_reshaped, axis=1, keepdims=True)  # Sum over neighbors (but here it's just one value per cell)
   100	        int_sum = nansum(weight_int_reshaped, axis=1, keepdims=True)
   101	        
   102	        # Actually, since we're processing one direction at a time, each cell gets one weight value
   103	        # So we don't need normalization here. Normalization happens later when combining all directions.
   104	        # But wait - that's not right either. Let me re-read the original algorithm.
   105	        
   106	        # define actual weight by using gamma, and weight components
   107	        weight = Particles.gamma * weight_sfc + (1 - Particles.gamma) * weight_int
   108	        
   109	        # modify the weight by the depth and theta weighting parameter
   110	        weight = neighbor_depth ** Particles.theta * weight
   111	        
   112	        # if the depth is below the minimum depth then location is not
   113	        # considered therefore set the associated weight to nan
   114	        weight[mask] = np.nan
   115	        
   116	        # Store in appropriate location in weight array
   117	        Particles.weight[1:-1, 1:-1, dir_idx] = weight
   118	    
   119	    # Now handle normalization and dead end cases properly
   120	    # Loop through interior cells to finalize weights
   121	    for i in range(1, L-1):
   122	        for j in range(1, W-1):
   123	            # Extract weights for this cell (all 9 directions)
   124	            cell_weights = Particles.weight[i, j, :]
   125	            
   126	            # Handle normalization for this cell
   127	            weight_sfc_full = np.zeros(9)
   128	            weight_int_full = np.zeros(9)
   129	            
   130	            # Recompute the full weights for normalization purposes
   131	            stage_ind = Particles.stage[i-1:i+2, j-1:j+2]
   132	            depth_ind = Particles.depth[i-1:i+2, j-1:j+2]
   133	            ct_ind = Particles.cell_type[i-1:i+2, j-1:j+2]
   134	            
   135	            # Surface slope weights
   136	            weight_sfc_full_raw = maximum(0, (Particles.stage[i, j]-stage_ind) / Particles.distances)
   137	            weight_int_full_raw = maximum(0, ((Particles.qx[i, j] * Particles.jvec +
   138	                                               Particles.qy[i, j] * Particles.ivec) /
   139	                                              Particles.distances))
   140	            
   141	            # Apply masks
   142	            mask_full = (depth_ind <= Particles.dry_depth) | (ct_ind == 2)
   143	            weight_sfc_full_raw[mask_full] = 0
   144	            weight_int_full_raw[mask_full] = 0
   145	            
   146	            # Normalize if needed
   147	            sfc_sum_total = nansum(weight_sfc_full_raw)
   148	            int_sum_total = nansum(weight_int_full_raw)
   149	            
   150	            if sfc_sum_total > 0:
   151	                weight_sfc_full = weight_sfc_full_raw / sfc_sum_total
   152	            else:
   153	                weight_sfc_full = weight_sfc_full_raw
   154	                
   155	            if int_sum_total > 0:
   156	                weight_int_full = weight_int_full_raw / int_sum_total
   157	            else:
   158	                weight_int_full = weight_int_full_raw
   159	            
   160	            # Combined weight
   161	            weight_full = Particles.gamma * weight_sfc_full + (1 - Particles.gamma) * weight_int_full
   162	            
   163	            # Apply depth weighting
   164	            weight_full = depth_ind.ravel() ** Particles.theta * weight_full
   165	            
   166	            # Apply final masking
   167	            weight_full[mask_full.ravel()] = np.nan
   168	            
   169	            # Handle dead end case
   170	            if nansum(weight_full) <= 0:
   171	                weight_full = np.zeros_like(weight_full)
   172	                if np.any(mask_full == False):  # If there are valid neighbors
   173	                    valid_depths = np.where(mask_full == False, depth_ind, -np.inf)
   174	                    max_flat_idx = np.argmax(valid_depths)
   175	                    weight_full[max_flat_idx] = 1.0
   176	            
   177	            # Store final weights
   178	            Particles.weight[i, j, :] = weight_full
   179	    
   180	    print('Finished routing weight calculation.')

    """Make an array with the routing weights."""
    # local namespace function imports
    from numpy import maximum
    from numpy import nansum
    # init the weight array
    L, W = np.shape(Particles.stage)
    Particles.weight = np.zeros((L, W, 9))
    
    # do weighting calculation for each cell
    print('Calculating routing weights ...')
    for i in tqdm(list(range(1, L-1)), ascii=True):
        for j in list(range(1, W-1)):
            # weights for each location in domain
            # get stage values for neighboring cells
            stage_ind = Particles.stage[i-1:i+2, j-1:j+2]

            # calculate surface slope weights
            weight_sfc = maximum(0,
                                 (Particles.stage[i, j]-stage_ind) /
                                 Particles.distances)

            # calculate inertial component weights
            weight_int = maximum(0, ((Particles.qx[i, j] * Particles.jvec +
                                      Particles.qy[i, j] * Particles.ivec) /
                                 Particles.distances))

            # get depth and cell types for neighboring cells
            depth_ind = Particles.depth[i-1:i+2, j-1:j+2]
            ct_ind = Particles.cell_type[i-1:i+2, j-1:j+2]

            # set weights for cells that are too shallow, or invalid 0
            weight_sfc[(depth_ind <= Particles.dry_depth) | (ct_ind == 2)] = 0
            weight_int[(depth_ind <= Particles.dry_depth) | (ct_ind == 2)] = 0

            # if sum of weights is above 0 normalize by sum of weights
            if nansum(weight_sfc) > 0:
                weight_sfc = weight_sfc / nansum(weight_sfc)

            # if sum of weight is above 0 normalize by sum of weights
            if nansum(weight_int) > 0:
                weight_int = weight_int / nansum(weight_int)

            # define actual weight by using gamma, and weight components
            weight = Particles.gamma * weight_sfc + \
                (1 - Particles.gamma) * weight_int

            # modify the weight by the depth and theta weighting parameter
            weight = depth_ind ** Particles.theta * weight

            # if the depth is below the minimum depth then location is not
            # considered therefore set the associated weight to nan
            weight[(depth_ind <= Particles.dry_depth) | (ct_ind == 2)] \
                = np.nan

            # if it's a dead end with only nans and 0's, choose deepest cell
            if nansum(weight) <= 0:
                weight = np.zeros_like(weight)
                weight[depth_ind == np.max(depth_ind)] = 1.0

            # set weight in the true weight array
            Particles.weight[i, j, :] = weight.ravel()

    print('Finished routing weight calculation.')</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>def make_weight(Particles):
    """Make an array with the routing weights."""
    # local namespace function imports
    from numpy import maximum
    from numpy import nansum
    # init the weight array
    L, W = np.shape(Particles.stage)
    Particles.weight = np.zeros((L, W, 9))
    
    # Define direction mappings for 3x3 neighborhood (flattened in row-major order)
    # Index 0-8 corresponds to: [-1,-1], [-1,0], [-1,1], [0,-1], [0,0], [0,1], [1,-1], [1,0], [1,1]
    di_indices = [-1, -1, -1,  0, 0, 0,  1, 1, 1]  # row offsets
    dj_indices = [-1,  0,  1, -1, 0, 1, -1, 0, 1]  # col offsets
    
    # do weighting calculation for each of the 9 directions
    print('Calculating routing weights ...')
    
    # Pre-compute center cell values (repeated for all directions)
    center_stage = Particles.stage[1:-1, 1:-1]
    center_qx = Particles.qx[1:-1, 1:-1]
    center_qy = Particles.qy[1:-1, 1:-1]
    center_depth = Particles.depth[1:-1, 1:-1]
    center_ct = Particles.cell_type[1:-1, 1:-1]
    
    # Process each direction
    for dir_idx in tqdm(list(range(9)), ascii=True):
        di, dj = di_indices[dir_idx], dj_indices[dir_idx]
        
        # Get neighbor values for this direction
        neighbor_stage = Particles.stage[1+di:L-1+di, 1+dj:W-1+dj]
        neighbor_depth = Particles.depth[1+di:L-1+di, 1+dj:W-1+dj]
        neighbor_ct = Particles.cell_type[1+di:L-1+di, 1+dj:W-1+dj]
        
        # Get distance and vector components for this direction
        # Map flat index to 2D coordinates in 3x3 grid
        i_local, j_local = divmod(dir_idx, 3)
        distance = Particles.distances[i_local, j_local]
        ivec_val = Particles.ivec[i_local, j_local]
        jvec_val = Particles.jvec[i_local, j_local]
        
        # calculate surface slope weights (global operation)
        weight_sfc = maximum(0, (center_stage - neighbor_stage) / distance)
        
        # calculate inertial component weights (global operation)
        weight_int = maximum(0, ((center_qx * jvec_val + center_qy * ivec_val) / distance))
        
        # set weights for cells that are too shallow, or invalid to 0
        mask = (neighbor_depth <= Particles.dry_depth) | (neighbor_ct == 2)
        weight_sfc[mask] = 0
        weight_int[mask] = 0
        
        # normalize by sum of weights if sum > 0 (per-cell normalization)
        # Need to reshape to add a dimension for proper broadcasting
        weight_sfc_reshaped = weight_sfc.reshape(weight_sfc.shape[0], weight_sfc.shape[1])
        weight_int_reshaped = weight_int.reshape(weight_int.shape[0], weight_int.shape[1])
        
        # Calculate sums for each cell
        sfc_sum = nansum(weight_sfc_reshaped, axis=1, keepdims=True)  # Sum over neighbors (but here it's just one value per cell)
        int_sum = nansum(weight_int_reshaped, axis=1, keepdims=True)
        
        # Actually, since we're processing one direction at a time, each cell gets one weight value
        # So we don't need normalization here. Normalization happens later when combining all directions.
        # But wait - that's not right either. Let me re-read the original algorithm.
        
        # define actual weight by using gamma, and weight components
        weight = Particles.gamma * weight_sfc + (1 - Particles.gamma) * weight_int
        
        # modify the weight by the depth and theta weighting parameter
        weight = neighbor_depth ** Particles.theta * weight
        
        # if the depth is below the minimum depth then location is not
        # considered therefore set the associated weight to nan
        weight[mask] = np.nan
        
        # Store in appropriate location in weight array
        Particles.weight[1:-1, 1:-1, dir_idx] = weight
    
    # Now handle normalization and dead end cases properly
    # Loop through interior cells to finalize weights
    for i in range(1, L-1):
        for j in range(1, W-1):
            # Extract weights for this cell (all 9 directions)
            cell_weights = Particles.weight[i, j, :]
            
            # Handle normalization for this cell
            weight_sfc_full = np.zeros(9)
            weight_int_full = np.zeros(9)
            
            # Recompute the full weights for normalization purposes
            stage_ind = Particles.stage[i-1:i+2, j-1:j+2]
            depth_ind = Particles.depth[i-1:i+2, j-1:j+2]
            ct_ind = Particles.cell_type[i-1:i+2, j-1:j+2]
            
            # Surface slope weights
            weight_sfc_full_raw = maximum(0, (Particles.stage[i, j]-stage_ind) / Particles.distances)
            weight_int_full_raw = maximum(0, ((Particles.qx[i, j] * Particles.jvec +
                                               Particles.qy[i, j] * Particles.ivec) /
                                              Particles.distances))
            
            # Apply masks
            mask_full = (depth_ind <= Particles.dry_depth) | (ct_ind == 2)
            weight_sfc_full_raw[mask_full] = 0
            weight_int_full_raw[mask_full] = 0
            
            # Normalize if needed
            sfc_sum_total = nansum(weight_sfc_full_raw)
            int_sum_total = nansum(weight_int_full_raw)
            
            if sfc_sum_total > 0:
                weight_sfc_full = weight_sfc_full_raw / sfc_sum_total
            else:
                weight_sfc_full = weight_sfc_full_raw
                
            if int_sum_total > 0:
                weight_int_full = weight_int_full_raw / int_sum_total
            else:
                weight_int_full = weight_int_full_raw
            
            # Combined weight
            weight_full = Particles.gamma * weight_sfc_full + (1 - Particles.gamma) * weight_int_full
            
            # Apply depth weighting
            weight_full = depth_ind.ravel() ** Particles.theta * weight_full
            
            # Apply final masking
            weight_full[mask_full.ravel()] = np.nan
            
            # Handle dead end case
            if nansum(weight_full) <= 0:
                weight_full = np.zeros_like(weight_full)
                if np.any(mask_full == False):  # If there are valid neighbors
                    valid_depths = np.where(mask_full == False, depth_ind, -np.inf)
                    max_flat_idx = np.argmax(valid_depths)
                    weight_full[max_flat_idx] = 1.0
            
            # Store final weights
            Particles.weight[i, j, :] = weight_full
    
    print('Finished routing weight calculation.')</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>str_replace</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>The file /workspace/passaH2O__dorado__2.4/dorado/lagrangian_walker.py has been edited. """Analyze the direction mapping to understand the indexing."""

import numpy as np

# Test array to understand ravel() indexing
test_array = np.arange(9).reshape(3, 3)
print("3x3 test array:")
print(test_array)
print("\nRaveled (row-major):")
print(test_array.ravel())

# Direction mapping
# Index in raveled array -> (row, col) in 3x3
directions = [
    (-1, -1),  # index 0
    (-1,  0),  # index 1  
    (-1,  1),  # index 2
    ( 0, -1),  # index 3
    ( 0,  0),  # index 4 (center)
    ( 0,  1),  # index 5
    ( 1, -1),  # index 6
    ( 1,  0),  # index 7
    ( 1,  1)   # index 8
]

print("\nDirection mapping:")
for i, (di, dj) in enumerate(directions):
    print(f"Index {i}: ({di}, {dj})")

# Verify with numpy functions
print("\nVerification with numpy:")
for i in range(9):
    row, col = divmod(i, 3)
    print(f"Index {i} -> row {row}, col {col}")</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>File created successfully at: /workspace/passaH2O__dorado__2.4/analyze_directions.py</tool_response><|assistant|></think><tool_call>execute_bash<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>cd /workspace/passaH2O__dorado__2.4 && python analyze_directions.py</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>3x3 test array:

Let me implement the global approach correctly:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/passaH2O__dorado__2.4/dorado/lagrangian_walker.py</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>def make_weight(Particles):
    """Make an array with the routing weights."""
    # local namespace function imports
    from numpy import maximum
    from numpy import nansum
    # init the weight array
    L, W = np.shape(Particles.stage)
    Particles.weight = np.zeros((L, W, 9))
    
    # Define direction mappings for 3x3 neighborhood (flattened in row-major order)
    # Index 0-8 corresponds to: [-1,-1], [-1,0], [-1,1], [0,-1], [0,0], [0,1], [1,-1], [1,0], [1,1]
    di_indices = [-1, -1, -1,  0, 0, 0,  1, 1, 1]  # row offsets
    dj_indices = [-1,  0,  1, -1, 0, 1, -1, 0, 1]  # col offsets
    
    # Pre-compute frequently used values for all interior cells
    center_i_slice = slice(1, L-1)
    center_j_slice = slice(1, W-1)
    center_stage = Particles.stage[center_i_slice, center_j_slice]
    center_qx = Particles.qx[center_i_slice, center_j_slice] 
    center_qy = Particles.qy[center_i_slice, center_j_slice]
    center_depth = Particles.depth[center_i_slice, center_j_slice]
    center_ct = Particles.cell_type[center_i_slice, center_j_slice]
    
    # Arrays to store weights for all directions and all cells
    all_weight_sfc = np.zeros((L-2, W-2, 9))
    all_weight_int = np.zeros((L-2, W-2, 9))
    all_neighbor_depth = np.zeros((L-2, W-2, 9))
    all_masks = np.zeros((L-2, W-2, 9), dtype=bool)
    
    # do weighting calculation for each of the 9 directions globally
    print('Calculating routing weights ...')
    
    for dir_idx in tqdm(list(range(9)), ascii=True):
        di, dj = di_indices[dir_idx], dj_indices[dir_idx]
        
        # Get neighbor values for this direction for ALL interior cells at once
        neighbor_i_slice = slice(1+di, L-1+di)
        neighbor_j_slice = slice(1+dj, W-1+dj)
        neighbor_stage = Particles.stage[neighbor_i_slice, neighbor_j_slice]
        neighbor_depth = Particles.depth[neighbor_i_slice, neighbor_j_slice]
        neighbor_ct = Particles.cell_type[neighbor_i_slice, neighbor_j_slice]
        
        # Get distance and vector components for this direction
        i_local, j_local = divmod(dir_idx, 3)
        distance = Particles.distances[i_local, j_local]
        ivec_val = Particles.ivec[i_local, j_local]
        jvec_val = Particles.jvec[i_local, j_local]
        
        # calculate surface slope weights (global operation across all interior cells)
        weight_sfc = maximum(0, (center_stage - neighbor_stage) / distance)
        
        # calculate inertial component weights (global operation across all interior cells)
        weight_int = maximum(0, ((center_qx * jvec_val + center_qy * ivec_val) / distance))
        
        # set weights for cells that are too shallow, or invalid to 0
        mask = (neighbor_depth <= Particles.dry_depth) | (neighbor_ct == 2)
        weight_sfc[mask] = 0
        weight_int[mask] = 0
        
        # Store computed values
        all_weight_sfc[:, :, dir_idx] = weight_sfc
        all_weight_int[:, :, dir_idx] = weight_int
        all_neighbor_depth[:, :, dir_idx] = neighbor_depth
        all_masks[:, :, dir_idx] = mask
    
    # Now perform per-cell normalization and final weight computation
    # This replaces the original cell-by-cell loop but operates on precomputed global arrays
    for i in range(L-2):
        for j in range(W-2):
            # Extract weights for this specific cell (all 9 directions)
            weight_sfc_cell = all_weight_sfc[i, j, :]
            weight_int_cell = all_weight_int[i, j, :]
            neighbor_depth_cell = all_neighbor_depth[i, j, :]
            mask_cell = all_masks[i, j, :]
            
            # Normalize surface weights if sum > 0
            sfc_sum = nansum(weight_sfc_cell)
            if sfc_sum > 0:
                weight_sfc_cell = weight_sfc_cell / sfc_sum
            
            # Normalize inertial weights if sum > 0  
            int_sum = nansum(weight_int_cell)
            if int_sum > 0:
                weight_int_cell = weight_int_cell / int_sum
            
            # define actual weight by using gamma, and weight components
            weight_cell = Particles.gamma * weight_sfc_cell + (1 - Particles.gamma) * weight_int_cell
            
            # modify the weight by the depth and theta weighting parameter
            weight_cell = neighbor_depth_cell ** Particles.theta * weight_cell
            
            # if the depth is below the minimum depth then location is not
            # considered therefore set the associated weight to nan
            weight_cell[mask_cell] = np.nan
            
            # if it's a dead end with only nans and 0's, choose deepest cell
            if nansum(weight_cell) <= 0:
                # Need to recreate the local neighborhood info for this cell
                global_i, global_j = i + 1, j + 1  # Convert back to global indices
                local_depth = Particles.depth[global_i-1:global_i+2, global_j-1:global_j+2]
                local_ct = Particles.cell_type[global_i-1:global_i+2, global_j-1:global_j+2]
                valid_mask = (local_depth > Particles.dry_depth) & (local_ct != 2)
                if np.any(valid_mask):
                    weight_cell = np.zeros_like(weight_cell)
                    valid_depths = np.where(valid_mask, local_depth, -np.inf)
                    max_flat_idx = np.argmax(valid_depths)
                    weight_cell[max_flat_idx] = 1.0
                else:
                    weight_cell = np.zeros_like(weight_cell)
            
            # set weight in the true weight array
            Particles.weight[i + 1, j + 1, :] = weight_cell
    
    print('Finished routing weight calculation.')</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>def make_weight(Particles):
    """Make an array with the routing weights."""
    # local namespace function imports
    from numpy import maximum
    from numpy import nansum
    # init the weight array
    L, W = np.shape(Particles.stage)
    Particles.weight = np.zeros((L, W, 9))
    
    # do weighting calculation for each cell
    print('Calculating routing weights ...')
    for i in tqdm(list(range(1, L-1)), ascii=True):
        for j in list(range(1, W-1)):
            # weights for each location in domain
            # get stage values for neighboring cells
            stage_ind = Particles.stage[i-1:i+2, j-1:j+2]

            # calculate surface slope weights
            weight_sfc = maximum(0,
                                 (Particles.stage[i, j]-stage_ind) /
                                 Particles.distances)

            # calculate inertial component weights
            weight_int = maximum(0, ((Particles.qx[i, j] * Particles.jvec +
                                      Particles.qy[i, j] * Particles.ivec) /
                                 Particles.distances))

            # get depth and cell types for neighboring cells
            depth_ind = Particles.depth[i-1:i+2, j-1:j+2]
            ct_ind = Particles.cell_type[i-1:i+2, j-1:j+2]

            # set weights for cells that are too shallow, or invalid 0
            weight_sfc[(depth_ind <= Particles.dry_depth) | (ct_ind == 2)] = 0
            weight_int[(depth_ind <= Particles.dry_depth) | (ct_ind == 2)] = 0

            # if sum of weights is above 0 normalize by sum of weights
            if nansum(weight_sfc) > 0:
                weight_sfc = weight_sfc / nansum(weight_sfc)

            # if sum of weight is above 0 normalize by sum of weights
            if nansum(weight_int) > 0:
                weight_int = weight_int / nansum(weight_int)

            # define actual weight by using gamma, and weight components
            weight = Particles.gamma * weight_sfc + \
                (1 - Particles.gamma) * weight_int

            # modify the weight by the depth and theta weighting parameter
            weight = depth_ind ** Particles.theta * weight

            # if the depth is below the minimum depth then location is not
            # considered therefore set the associated weight to nan
            weight[(depth_ind <= Particles.dry_depth) | (ct_ind == 2)] \
                = np.nan

            # if it's a dead end with only nans and 0's, choose deepest cell
            if nansum(weight) <= 0:
                weight = np.zeros_like(weight)
                weight[depth_ind == np.max(depth_ind)] = 1.0

            # set weight in the true weight array
            Particles.weight[i, j, :] = weight.ravel()

    print('Finished routing weight calculation.')</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>str_replace</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>The file /workspace/passaH2O__dorado__2.4/dorado/lagrangian_walker.py has been edited.     def test_wet_boundary_no_weight():
        '''
        Confirm that even if the boundary cell is deep enough, its weight == 0.
        '''
